  Separately, **the changelog was never actually being read.** Its content is server-rendered, so `flight_chunks()` yielded an 832-character empty shell while the plain markup carried every release — a parser waiting on a Markdown or JSON endpoint would report no entries and no error. `changelog_entries()` now strips tags and parses the `[date] // v<version> <product> FEAT…` structure, so the check reads **what** shipped independently of **whether** the version moved: the catalog and plan pages legitimately move without a CLI release. What's New carries a different, version-less shape and is kept as raw prose for review rather than forced through that parser.


- Reuse serialized bodies and prepared images during pre-stream account rotation; bound ordinary transient failures to three retries while retaining separate usage-window and transport budgets.
- Scope learned and catalog-published output limits, billing access, and protocol choices to `apiBase`. Disk catalog cache version 3 records its gateway and rejects unscoped or mismatched files.
- **The consecutive-header-timeout streak was shared across every concurrent session on the process, not scoped to one.** `CommandCodeAdapter` is registered once and reused for every agent, but `headerTimeoutStreak` lived as a single instance field rather than the `WeakMap<agent, …>` pattern the new transient/transport retry budgets use. Two failure modes followed from that: a success on ANY session cleared every other session's in-progress streak (the escalation to a terminal diagnosis after `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_HEADER_TIMEOUTS` almost never fired under concurrency), and, since the streak also compares a body fingerprint, two sessions repeatedly timing out on different request shapes kept resetting each other's count back to 1 (same effect, opposite mechanism). The field is now `headerTimeoutStreaks`, a `Map` keyed by `GenerateOptions.sessionId` (not `agent`: `GenerateOptions` carries no agent reference) with FIFO eviction past 200 entries; a session-less call keeps the old single-slot behavior via a fixed fallback key. `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins independent per-session accumulation and the session-less fallback.

- **Claude sessions were billed at the full input rate on every turn — the Messages route never asked for a cache.** Anthropic's prompt caching is opt-in per content block: a prefix is stored only when a `cache_control` marker names it, unlike the automatic prefix caching the OpenAI-shaped routes get. The adapter set exactly one marker on the whole plugin, on the CLI route's system section. The Messages route — the only route the entire Claude family answers on — sent **no marker at all**, so every turn re-prefilled the whole replayed history at the undiscounted input price. A 10-turn `claude-sonnet-5-5` session measured 2026-09-29 billed **$0.5648**, and each of its ten requests matched the undiscounted formula to the cent (`37898×$2/M + 1312×$10/M = $0.088916` against a logged `$0.0889`), which is what establishes the zero rather than any display bug — one cached token anywhere in the session would have moved the total. `mapMessagesUsage()` was already reading `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` correctly, so the counters were faithfully reporting a zero the request had asked for.

  The body now carries **three ephemeral breakpoints** — the `system` block, the LAST tool declaration, and the last block of the last `user` message — for **$0.1869 on that same session, a 66.9% reduction**, with no other field changed. The last user turn is the rolling boundary: DSH resends the full history each turn, so a marker there grows with the conversation, while a `system`-only marker would leave every turn's history uncached. `messagesMessageCodec` renders tool results as `role: 'user'`, so a tool loop's trailing turn is marked too; `thinking` and `tool_use` blocks never are, since a breakpoint there caches a prefix the next turn invalidates. `system` becomes a one-block array on this route only — the same shape the CLI route has always sent, and the only way a string field could carry a marker. The CLI and OpenAI routes are untouched, and `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins both.

  **The placements are copied from the official client, not invented.** Command Code ships
  [`pi-commandcode-provider`](https://github.com/CommandCodeAI/pi-commandcode-provider), which calls this
  same `/provider/v1/messages` endpoint through `@earendil-works/pi-ai` and hand-writes no marker at all:
  that library's `anthropic-messages` transport marks precisely these three positions, and reads
  `pricing.cache_read` / `pricing.cache_write` from the model list to price the result. Both artifacts were
  checked against their registry-published sha512 digests (`pi-ai` 0.87.1, `command-code` 1.68.0, both
  matching) rather than taken on trust, and `command-code` 1.68.0 separately shows the CLI marks only its own
  system section and never touches this endpoint — which is why the CLI-route cache regressions in
  [docs/issue-64-cache-review.md](docs/issue-64-cache-review.md) say nothing about this one. **This could
  not be confirmed against a live Command Code response** — the account had no remaining quota — so the
  claim that the gateway honours the field rests on the official provider depending on it for its own cost
  display, not on a measured `cache_read_input_tokens`. The downside is bounded either way: a gateway that
  ignored the field would leave price and shape exactly as they are, and one that honoured it without a hit
  would bill the first turn's write at $2.5/M against $2/M, with every later turn hitting inside the
  5-minute window.

- **A lone UTF-16 surrogate half in any outgoing string is now removed before it reaches the wire.** A surrogate half is not a character — it comes from a truncated slice, a lossy decode, or a stray `\uD83D` escape — and `JSON.stringify` does **not** reject it: it serializes to the escape `"\ud83d"` and leaves the process as a string no UTF-8 consumer can round-trip. Whether that is fatal depends entirely on the receiving decoder, and the official provider for pi strips these from every outgoing string on this same endpoint, which is the evidence this change rests on. The strip is applied through the `JSON.stringify` replacer at the **single point a request body is serialized**, so all three transports and every field — system text, message content, tool schemas, tool results — are covered at once, rather than enumerating the fields that can carry text and missing one. **This is defensive, not a reproduced crash**: measured locally, `JSON.stringify` accepts a lone surrogate without complaint and the bytes stay valid UTF-8, so the failure it guards against is a strict upstream decoder, which could not be exercised without quota.

  Two details are load-bearing. **Paired surrogates must survive** — a well-formed astral character IS a surrogate pair, so a naive range filter would delete every emoji; `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins that both a lone half and an emoji are handled correctly. And the **exact** pattern needs lookaround, which V8 cannot turn into a fast scan: on a 412 KB request body it costs 1.40 ms against a bare `JSON.stringify` of 0.59 ms. A flagless range pre-test gates it, because real prompts are ASCII-plus-CJK with no surrogates at all, bringing the ordinary case to 0.77 ms. The pre-test deliberately uses a **non-global** regex: a global one is stateful, and testing with it would advance `lastIndex` so consecutive calls on the same string alternated between true and false.

- **A model that refuses `max_tokens` now costs one wasted request, not a failed turn — and the failure is no longer blamed on the context.** Issue #71: `claude-sonnet-5-5` sends `max_tokens: 131072` (the global default, which is 128 × 1024) against a real ceiling of 128 000, and the Messages endpoint refuses it by name. Two things went wrong, and the first one hid the second. The refusal text contains the word `max_tokens`, which `CLI_CONTEXT_OVERFLOW_PATTERN` matches, so it was classified `CONTEXT_WINDOW_EXCEEDED` — the harness then compacted a session that was not oversized, resent the **identical** request, was refused identically, and told the user to start a new conversation that could not possibly work. `OUTPUT_LIMIT_REJECTION` was already claiming those shapes for the HTTP path; the fix here extends the same ordering to `streamErrorToLlmError()`, which is the path issue #71 actually reported (`Command Code stream error: …`, a 200 body carrying an in-stream error frame, not a 400).

  The refusal is now **retried once at the ceiling the endpoint states**: `streamRequest()` wraps `streamAttempt()` and, when a failure arrives before the first chunk reaches the host, parses Y out of `max_tokens: X > Y`, records it and re-sends. Both shapes are covered because the recovery is identical for each. What makes it pay off is the record — `learnedOutputLimits` is process-wide and only ever tightens, so the turn that *is* refused still leaves every later turn for that model built correctly, without spending a 400 to find out again. A refusal arriving after a delta has been delivered is not retried (the answer is already partly on screen; a second request would duplicate it), and a second refusal within one turn surfaces, so this is one downshift per turn and not a loop. `streamAttempt()` now takes the smaller of the catalog row and the learned value, so a warm catalog cannot mask a limit the gateway has already stated.

- **The Messages route no longer halves every Opus answer to avoid a refusal it can now predict.** With the ceiling known, the `claude-*` fallback of 64 000 that predates the table is no longer needed for the family it was written for. `MODEL_OUTPUT_TOKEN_LIMITS` is now **generated** by `scripts/sync-output-limits.mjs` from models.dev rather than hand-maintained from whatever 400 a user happened to hit, and it covers **74 of the 84 catalog ids** — not only the Claude and GPT families, but the open-weight models too, because Command Code sells those through their **vendor's own API** and the vendor's published ceiling is the one worth writing. The script resolves it in order: the vendor's own catalog entry (matched on the normalized id tail, since Command Code namespaces `Qwen/Qwen3.8-Max` while the vendor files `qwen3.8-max`), the same entry with a `free` suffix dropped — both spellings occur, `inclusionai/ling-3.0-flash-sante:free` and `poolside/laguna-s-2.1-free` — and finally unanimity across every provider carrying the id.

  An earlier pass at this took the opposite line: it wrote a value only where every provider agreed, and left all 48 open-weight models to runtime learning. That was over-cautious in a way that cost real capability. A cross-provider aggregation disagrees precisely because each host configures its own deployment — `moonshotai/Kimi-K3` carries nine `output` values from 8000 to 1048576 across 26 providers — so unanimity was never going to arrive for exactly the models that needed an answer, and "take the minimum" would have cut Kimi to 8000 tokens. The vendor figure is the correct one to start from, and when the gateway is stricter than the vendor the runtime correction above handles it: a ceiling that is too high costs one round trip, while a conservative guess costs half of every answer for as long as the table stands. The 10 models no vendor publishes yet (`Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B`, `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, `moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5`, `zai-org/GLM-5.2-Fast`, `thinkingmachines/inkling-small`, two `tencent/hy*`, three id-only rows) stay unlisted and are learned the same way.

- **Messages asked a non-reasoning model to reason.** `thinking: { type: 'adaptive' }` was sent unconditionally, but that switch exists to be driven by `output_config.effort` — the endpoint's own wording is "Use `thinking.type.adaptive` AND `output_config.effort` to control thinking behavior". `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` has neither: no effort levels, and the official CLI's model table marks it neither `reasoning_effort` nor `reasoning:!0`, so it does not reason. It was being handed half of a pair that controls nothing. The switch now travels only when `KNOWN_EFFORTS` publishes levels for the model (reusing the lookup `stream()` already performs), leaving nine of the ten Claude models untouched. **This one could not be confirmed against a live response**: every request for that model stops at 403 `MODEL_NOT_IN_PLAN` on a GOAT-plan tier, before generation, so it rests on the official model table plus the endpoint's field validation — the switch is accepted there, it is simply meaningless. Related, and measured: the switch does **not** suppress thinking (six live requests across `adaptive` present/absent × `temperature` present/absent all produced a thinking block), so an apparent difference between two probe runs was the `max` vs `xhigh` effort rather than the switch.

- **Messages never sends `temperature` — adaptive thinking refuses any other value.** The body builder copied the other two transports' `temperature: options.temperature ?? 0.3`, and the endpoint answers `` `temperature` may only be set to 1 when thinking is enabled or in adaptive mode ``. **Every real request was refused with HTTP 400 while all 722 unit tests passed**: the failure only exists on the wire, which is what `scripts/verify-messages-live.mjs` now exists to catch. Omission is the only field state that satisfies both the thinking switch and a caller that asked for a temperature, so Claude loses sampling control on this route because the endpoint removed it. `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins the absence across `temperature` values 0 / 0.3 / 1 / 2.

- **A `max_tokens` rejection no longer triggers an expensive, useless compaction.** The context-overflow pattern matches a bare `max_tokens`, so both a ceiling refusal and a plain validation error on that field were reported as `CONTEXT_WINDOW_EXCEEDED` — which hands the turn to `dsh-compaction-basic`, and the harness then compacts a session that was never oversized, at full price on a long context, and is refused identically on the retry. `OUTPUT_LIMIT_REJECTION` now claims the request-side shapes first and returns a bilingual `PROVIDER_HTTP_ERROR` quoting the endpoint's own numbers, so the user learns which setting to lower instead of watching a compaction that cannot help.


## [0.12.0] - 2026-09-29

### Added

- **The model list now shows how far each model stretches your plan: its monthly allowance.** The pricing page publishes a **per-model allowance** (its own term) alongside every model's rates — "$47 of monthly usage on GOAT, $57 on Pro", to quote its MiniMax M3 example, which is the exact pair the new snapshot carries. The settings page's model dropdowns (Visible models, and the per-account routing pickers, which share one component) now render it beside the model name as `$20/mo`, with a tooltip spelling out what it is. **It is not extra credit**: every allowance draws on the same monthly pool, so `$20/mo` means "this model may consume $20 of that pool", not "$20 added to it". Two facts shape the implementation, both from the page rather than from guesswork: the figure exists for **GOAT and Pro only** ("the boost is a per-model allowance, so it lives on the plans that have them"), and deals are **already baked in** — MiniMax M3's `$47 / $57` IS its 2× promotion, folded in upstream. So `allowanceTierForWeight()` maps a subscription tier weight onto those two brackets and answers `undefined` for Go / Provider / Max / Ultra, and the Host resolves the bracket from the POOL's highest plan (`adapter.allowanceTier()`), matching the question `modelVisibleForAnyAccount` already answers for a pool. A Go or Max account therefore sees no allowance at all rather than a GOAT number — the honest outcome, and the reason the badge is absent rather than zero. The data rides the price rows (`allowance: { goat, pro }`, same page record, same slug resolution as the rates) and reaches the browser through the existing `commandcode/models` Remote as a single Host-resolved number; the wire stays a plain `allowance?: number` so the browser never re-derives the bracket. `tests/model-prices.test.ts` checks the snapshot against the page's own prose (MiniMax M3 at `$47 / $57`), asserts every priced catalog model carries both brackets, and pins that only weights 1 and 2 map; `tests/adapter.test.ts` covers the pool's highest-plan rule and its fail-open shape when billing is unreadable.

- **One accessibility fix on the way past: badges are no longer below the 12 px floor.** The shared `.cc-badge` / `.cc-badgeMuted` rule rendered at `font-size: 11px`. It is now 12px, the minimum the repository's visual rules allow for labels and auxiliary text. Only that one rule changed — the remaining sub-12px declarations in `panel-styles.ts` and the usage-plan pill are untouched and still need a decision.

### Fixed

- **A header timeout now names its real cause, and stops being retried forever (#67).** The single message "通常是网络或代理问题，请检查后重试" was wrong for both transports and sent the reporter of #67 to their proxy while their gateway was simply not answering. Measured 2026-09-28, keeping **headers** separate from **totals** (a total includes the model producing every token and says nothing about the header wait). Headers, with `max_tokens: 16` to collapse the generation, 26 250-token prompt: `/alpha/generate` 2.12 / 2.56 / 2.75 s, `/provider/v1/chat/completions` 5.52 / 2.98 / 3.70 s — a ~1.1 s median gap, with the Provider API route swinging 2.5 s where the CLI route moved 0.6 s. Totals at `max_tokens: 1024` were 11.1 s / 17.9 s for the CLI route and 116.7 s / 30.5 s for the Provider API route at 200 k and 1.2 M chars — the 200 k prompt finishing 10× slower than the 1.2 M one is why a size-based rule was wrong, and it is also why the 116.7 s figure is not header evidence. So the message branches on the transport: the CLI route names provider queueing, Go-plan capacity or a network/proxy path and says a longer budget will not help; the Provider API route names the upstream first token and suggests a concrete `requestTimeoutMs` (doubled, floored at 5 minutes, capped at the retry backoff's own 15). Both say outright that prompt size is not the cause. Separately, three consecutive header timeouts on one unchanged payload now leave the retryable whitelist as `PROVIDER_HTTP_ERROR` with an explanation, because dsh-llm exposes a single scalar `maxRetries` and no per-code limit, and `maxRetries: 1000` with a 500 ms → 15 min backoff meant the identical multi-megabyte body was re-sent for hours instead of producing a diagnosis. `RATE_LIMIT` / `SERVER` / `TRANSPORT` keep retrying — each carries a real "come back later" signal — and any answer from the gateway ends the streak. The "Request timeout" hint in the Advanced card now says when the Provider API route needs a bigger budget, and **the default moved from 60 s to 300 s to match the official CLI**. That number is the CLI's, not a round choice: decompiling `command-code@1.66.0` shows its `createNodeTransport` fetch passes only the caller's `signal` and sets no timeout at all, so a model request inherits Node/undici's own default — measured at 301.0 s against a local server that accepts the connection and never sends headers. Being stricter than the CLI only ever costs legitimate requests, since the Provider API route charges its upstream's time-to-first-token to the header wait. The trade is that a genuinely dead gateway now takes longer to name itself, which is what the streak is for.

- **The output budget was raised from 64 000 to 131 072.** It was never a provider requirement: probing `/alpha/generate` directly, 64 000 → 200, 131 072 → 200, 196 608 → 200, **200 000 → 200, 200 704 → 400**, 1 000 000 → 400. The server caps `max_tokens` at 200 000, and identically so for a 262 144-window model and a 1 000 000-window one — the cap is global, not per model — so 64 000 was using 32 % of what the provider allows, and the 384 000 figure quoted elsewhere would be refused. 131 072 keeps a 35 % margin under the measured ceiling while covering the real output limits of the models that declare one (Claude 64 K, Gemini 64 K, several at 128 K). It cost nothing in latency to leave at 64 000 (a 64 000 request answered headers in 1.28 s against 1.57 s for 131 072) but it did cap what a model could say in one turn. `DEFAULT_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` moved with it: `/provider/v1/models` publishes only `context_length` and no output ceiling, so the catalog derives a stand-in from the window, and leaving that fallback lower would have re-capped every request before the ceiling was ever consulted. Per request, `requestContextBudget` still lowers the budget from the prompt actually being sent, so raising the ceiling cannot push a large session over its window.

- **Both timeout fields are edited in seconds, not milliseconds.** The settings page asked users to do arithmetic to answer "how long is a reasonable wait" — the defaults were `300000` and the ceiling `2147483647`. The stored unit is unchanged (milliseconds, the Host schema, and switching it would break every existing profile); this is presentation only, formatted and parsed through a new `secondsField` spec. Inputs are whole seconds from 1 to 3600 — past an hour a header wait is a hung process, not a budget, and the input is the place to refuse it. Fractions are refused rather than rounded, so the box and the saved profile cannot disagree, and a profile hand-edited beyond the range still displays its true number instead of being silently clamped. The hints now state the default in seconds, and a test reads `DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` to keep the copy from drifting away from the constant.

- **CLI image requests now match the official final wire shape (#64).** `/alpha/generate` sends image data URLs with `mimeType`, and both chat transports label each request image with the media type returned by `readImageRequest()` after any JPEG/WebP transcode. Tests cover tool-result images, stored-PNG/request-JPEG variants and stable historical image messages when another image is appended. Live calls showed that this correction alone does not prevent the provider's cache retreat when another image arrives.

### Changed

- **Updated the exact DeepSeek Harness pairing from `0.1.7-rc.2` to `0.2.0-rc.1`.** Every Harness peer and development range, `dsh.compatibility.dsh`, `engines.dsh`, and the single `dshReleases` record now name 0.2.0-rc.1 together, and no business code changed: the release audit over the full public source delta (1111 changed paths, 322 package manifests) found 13 of the 16 Harness packages with **zero source changes** — `dsh-llm`, `dsh-settings`, `dsh-typert-protocol`, `dsh-credentials`, `dsh-commands`, `dsh-web`, `dsh-timeout`, `dsh-agent`, `dsh-attachment`, `dsh-launch-environment`, `dsh-invariants`, `cordis` and the two client-only rows — so `GenerateOptions`/`RequestMessage`/`ContentBlock`/`LlmError` and the settings `volatile` contract are byte-identical. The three that moved are additive: `dsh-api-remotes/client` adds one `productAnalyticsRemote` contribution to its assembly list, and `dsh-client-ui-primitives` adds one `pointerModality` export. All five slot ids this plugin registers (`settings.section`, `settings.models.provider-card`, `settings.models.footer`, `sidebar.footer.action`, `conversation.composer.dock`) survive in the new slot catalog with an unchanged `slot-contract.ts`, `apps/web` has no source change so the platform seed table is untouched, and the nine `staticModules` seeds were re-read out of the 0.2.0-rc.1 frontend and are identical to 0.1.7-rc.2's — the argument for keeping `react`, `dsh-client-ui-primitives` and `dsh-client-ui-slots` optional still holds. The `data-composer-stats` outlet the cost readout attaches to is still present at its original site. `npm run test:engine` passes against a real `@deepseek-ai/dsh@0.2.0-rc.1` (ESM link, every static named import, the client `require()` seed audit, image pricing, the volatile Config, tool history on both transports, and the durable offload contract). Deliberately not adopted: the optional `dsh-experimental-schedule-bundle` that automation tasks moved into, the desktop-only product-analytics telemetry, and `resolveAccountToken` in the official `web-search-deepseek` — this plugin ships its own `web_search` provider over the same credential chain and gains nothing from it.

- **Opt-in cache-aware image history for the CLI transport (#64).** `offloadSeenImagesForCache` asks dsh's durable image-offload surface to replace older images only after the same model has answered with them. A four-request live A/B showed that the next image then retained the growing text prefix instead of reverting to the first image boundary. It is off by default because old pixels are unavailable until reread or reattached; this is a mitigation, not a provider-side cache fix.

- **Internal cleanup with no behavior change: dead-code removal, comment trimming, and small refactors.** Removed the unreferenced `isPeakHour()` helper (the readout prices through `cost-facts.ts` and `capabilities.ts`; its test is replaced by one that pins billing-vs-picker peak agreement across every weekday boundary), inlined the single-use `StatTile` wrapper, dropped the never-used `AccountReport summaryOnly` mode, narrowed the mirror's internal status union to the states it actually writes, and registered the login endpoints through `LOGIN_HOST_CONTRIBUTION` instead of spreading the raw descriptors. Trimmed duplicated design narratives in `transport-retry.ts`, `accounts.ts`, and the slot-declaration files (one canonical copy each). Small performance refactors: `costGroupKey()` now resolves prices through a per-table index instead of scanning the table per call, and the cost projection builds its group lookup in one pass. Typecheck clean, full suite green (679 tests).

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.67.0 and 1.68.0 CLIs.** `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` and the `x-command-code-version` header now report `1.68.0`. Both releases landed within hours of each other (npm `1.67.0` at 2026-09-28T21:39Z, `1.68.0` at 2026-09-28T23:13Z) and **the official changelog page still documents neither** — its newest entry is 1.66.0 (2026-09-26) — so the integrity-verified tarballs are the evidence, and the two releases were separated by diffing the pinned 1.66.0 artifact, the 1.67.0 artifact and `latest` side by side. 1.67.0 adds **`deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-fast`** (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash Fast): `/chat/completions` + `/responses`, 1M context, text+image, `low`/`high`/`max` efforts, **Go**-tier, hourly peak/off-peak pricing at its own $0.16/$0.58/$0.016 off-peak and $0.32/$1.16/$0.032 peak — deliberately **not** 2× its `deepseek-v4.1-flash` sibling's rates, so only that row's own `timeOfDay` block is the evidence — and it is **not** ZDR-covered. 1.68.0 adds **`claude-sonnet-5-5`** (Claude Sonnet 5.5): `/messages`, 1M context, text+image, the five-level effort set, **GOAT and above**, at $2.00/$10.00/$0.20/$2.50 — one tier *above* its `claude-sonnet-5` predecessor, which keeps its Pro slot. 1.68.0 also adds one non-model feature, the TUI setting **"Show Taste learning in feed"** (`showTasteLearningInFeed`, boolean, user scope, default on), which hides Taste notices from the feed without disabling learning; it lives in the CLI's own config and has no counterpart in this plugin. Everything else is unchanged and was re-verified rather than assumed: the subscription plan maps, the ZDR anchor sets beyond the new row, the API route table, the `x-command-code-version` / `x-cmd-zdr` header table, the account-rejection vocabulary and the stream event names are byte-identical across 1.66.0 → 1.68.0. The remaining artifact diff is minified identifier rotation (e.g. `kR` → `CR`), which carries no behavior.

- **The capability snapshot grew by two models, and the vendored price table with it.** `KNOWN_EFFORTS` 53 → 55, `KNOWN_IMAGE_MODELS` 62 → 64, `KNOWN_PLANS` 82 → 84 (Go 52 → 53, GOAT 8 → 9 more; the cumulative chain is now **53/62/76/84** against a catalog that serves 84 models at 66 × chat/completions + responses, 10 Claude ids × messages, 8 × chat/completions), `KNOWN_PEAK_PRICING` 4 → 5, and `KNOWN_NON_ZDR_MODELS` 22 → 23. `scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs` rewrote `MODEL_PRICE_ROWS` with exactly two new rows and no other change, which is also what keeps `tests/model-prices.test.ts`'s "every catalog model is either priced or explicitly free" invariant green. `tests/zdr.test.ts` pins the new membership deliberately, `tests/adapter.test.ts` gained the two models' effort/plan/Vision/peak assertions and its version constant moved with the header.

- **One provenance note was corrected, not carried forward.** The `stealth/pixel-canary` comment claimed it entered the ZDR exclusion set "through `modelSupportsZdr` alone", and the `KNOWN_NON_ZDR_MODELS` docstring repeated that both stealth previews were missing from the sibling route table. Re-reading all three artifacts (1.66.0 / 1.67.0 / 1.68.0) on 2026-09-29 shows the row is listed in **both** anchor sets in every one of them; `meituan/LongCat-2.0` is the only genuine divergence. Membership never depended on this — the snapshot is the union — but the explanation was wrong and is now the evidence it claims to be.

- **A lapsed discount no longer prices a session at the promotional rate.** The picker has always hidden an expired deal, but the vendored price table kept charging its discount: Grok 4.7's 40%-off launch promotion ended `2026-09-27T23:59:59.999Z`, yet the composer still quoted **$1.20 / $3.60 / $0.30** against a row the page reverts to **$2.00 / $6.00 / $0.50** — a 40% under-report on every session, with the >200K band off by the same factor. The page publishes both sets (the promotional figures as the row's own rates, the pre-discount `listRates` alongside), so `scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs` now copies both and `modelPriceTable(now)` picks between them on **`dealLabel()`'s own clock**. Badge and dollars therefore flip on the same instant, and both context bands flip together so a >200K request is never priced off a mixed pair. The table is rebuilt per request, so the switch needs no redeploy; a deal with no `expiresAt` (the two MiMo rows, MiniMax M3) never lapses and keeps its promotional rates. `tests/model-prices.test.ts` pins the revert, the shared boundary (inclusive on the expiry side) and the no-expiry case.

- **The page's `discountPercent` is not the revert factor, and the script no longer pretends it is.** Dividing the promotional rates by `1 - pct/100` looks obvious and is wrong: MiMo V2.5 is advertised at "98% off" while its published list is `0.8 / 4 / 0.16` against charged `0.14 / 0.28 / 0.0028` — ratios of 5.7×, 14.3× and 57×, not one percentage. The generator therefore copies the page's literal `listRates` verbatim, scales a context tier only by that row's own per-bucket ratios, and prints a warning instead of guessing when those disagree. A deal with **no** literal `listRates` is left alone deliberately: the page is saying the unit price does not change (Qwen 3.7 Max's `2x-usage` doubles the allowance, it does not discount a token). Rows whose list equals their charged rates (typesafe/jev) get no fallback data, so nothing dormant ships.

- **Fixed, same day: the price script's HTML cross-check had been blind since the page redesign.** `renderedRates()` split on `<tr>`/`<td>`, but the model table is now a `<div>` grid — a live run extracted **6 rows out of 88 models**, and those six were the plan cards (Go/GOAT/Pro/Max 10×/Max 20×/Team Pro). Every model row had therefore been rewritten from the embedded JSON alone with no second opinion, which is the exact safety net that once caught `deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp` shipping ~47% high. The parser now anchors on the row's name cell (`flex min-w-0 items-center gap-2 px-4 py-3`, whose display name is marked `text-[13px] font-medium` — a deal badge next to it uses `text-[10px]`, so they never collide) and harvests **88 of 88** rows. Re-running the generator on the fixed parser produced a **byte-identical table**, which is what makes the previous write trustworthy in hindsight rather than merely unchecked. Two guards came out of it: `main()` now **exits 2 instead of rewriting** when the harvest falls below half the record count, so the next layout change fails loudly rather than comparing nothing; and a discounted row's struck-through `<s>` figures are compared against the table JSON's `listRates`, giving the pre-discount copy an independent rendering to agree with. The `STRUCK_RATE` regex is deliberately `<s(?:\s[^>]*)?>`: the obvious `<s[^>]*>` also matches `<span …>`, which read the DeepSeek rows' tooltip spans as struck-through prices during development. Rows showing a word (`Free`) or an em-dash publish no figure and are skipped — an unparseable row is never counted as agreement.

## [0.11.17] - 2026-09-27

### Changed

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.66.0 CLI.** `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` and the `x-command-code-version` header now report `1.66.0`. The release is additive with exactly one change against `1.65.5` (the newest version the official changelog page still documents, so the bundle diff is the evidence): the CLI registry gains `stealth/pixel-canary` (Pixel Canary) — Vercel AI Gateway-served, text+image, 262,144-token context, reasoning with the selectable `low`/`medium`/`high` set, available on every plan from Go up, and **free while the stealth preview lasts**. The public catalog serves it too (82 models), on `/chat/completions` + `/responses`. It is the **second change to the ZDR exception set since the 1.62.0 check**: it enters through `modelSupportsZdr` alone (the 1.66.0 bundle's `knownModelSupportsZdr` set does not repeat it), the pricing page says it plainly ("Free while the preview lasts. Not routed under ZDR."), so `KNOWN_NON_ZDR_MODELS` grows 21 → 22 and `tests/zdr.test.ts` pins the new membership deliberately. It is also the second free stealth model to carry no vendored price row, exactly like Space Bunny Alpha — the price table's free-model branch prices it at $0. **The wire protocol is untouched**: the 26 `/alpha/*` and `/provider/v1/*` routes, the `x-command-code-version` / `x-cmd-zdr` header table, the account-rejection code vocabulary, the stream event names and the subscription-plan maps are the same set as in 1.65.5. Everything else was re-verified against the live catalog and the pricing/docs pages on 2026-09-27 and is unchanged: the catalog's 82 models and its 65 × chat/completions + responses / 9 Claude ids × messages / 8 × chat/completions route mix, the plan map at 52/60/74/82, the full effort map, the Vision set, every other deal, and the DeepSeek peak schedule.

- **Two vendored price rows moved.** The official pricing page lowers Step 3.5 Flash's input rate from $0.10 to **$0.09** per 1M tokens, and publishes a `typesafe/jev` row the page did not carry before. Neither changes what the picker shows: Step 3.5 Flash's badge and plan tier are unchanged, and `typesafe/jev` is not in the public catalog and is not served by either chat transport — the System One decision client that used to price it is removed in this same release, so the row simply keeps the table in step with the page. One promotion is on its own clock rather than in this table: Grok 4.7's 40%-off deal lapses at `2026-09-27T23:59:59Z`, at which point the picker badge hides itself and the row returns to the $2.00 / $6.00 / $0.50 list rates on the next sync. Re-run `node scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs` to pick up either move.

### Fixed

- **The opt-in stream trace now fingerprints the request, not just the response.** `DSH_COMMANDCODE_TRACE` recorded the raw response chunks and how the stream ended, but nothing about what was asked, so a provider cache hit or miss could not be tied to a request shape. Every attempt now appends a `request` record before it is sent — transport, model, thread id, the message and tool counts, and a SHA-256 of the body, config, system prompt, tool list and message list. It is the same privacy contract as the rest of the trace (counts and hashes only, never the text, and a record that cannot be written never fails the request it observes), and it is skipped entirely when the trace is off, so the default path stays one environment read. `tests/stream-trace.test.ts` pins the record on both a finished request and a pre-stream failure, and asserts the prompt text is absent from the file.

- CLI requests now derive a stable UUID thread ID from DSH's prefixed session IDs, so successive requests in an ordinary session keep the same provider thread ID. This preserves request correlation; a cache-hit improvement still requires live evidence.

### Removed

- **The AI command guard is gone; dsh's own approval tooling does this job now.** The opt-in `commandGuard` / `commandGuardLevel` settings, the `approval/request` answerer and the `tools/pre-execute` observer it registered, the `typesafe/jev` decision client for `POST /provider/v1/systemone`, their offline explainer script, and the settings-page / dsh-TUI controls and copy are all removed. The plugin no longer touches the `approval` seam at all and no longer calls the System One decision endpoint — Command Code chat, web search, cost readouts, accounts and ZDR are unaffected. A stored `commandGuard` / `commandGuardLevel` value in an existing profile is simply ignored (schemastery keeps unknown keys without validating them), so nothing needs migrating.

## [0.11.16] - 2026-09-25

### Changed

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.65.2 CLI.** `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` and the `x-command-code-version` header now report `1.65.2`. The public catalog already exposed Step 3.5 Flash's corrected 262,144-token context, so no picker or price row moves; the full effort, Vision, automatic-reasoning, plan, subscription, deal, peak-pricing and ZDR snapshots remain unchanged.

- **Updated the exact DeepSeek Harness pairing from `0.1.7-rc.1` to `0.1.7-rc.2`.** Every Harness peer and development range, `dsh.compatibility.dsh`, `engines.dsh`, and the single `dshReleases` record now name rc.2 together. The rc.2 client graph no longer carries the obsolete `dsh-client-connection` edge or peer; the browser bundle still exports only the live `slots`, `locale`, and `remote` service face. `npm run test:engine` is the compatibility proof for this exact pairing.

### Added

- **Command Code models can receive newly enabled tools during an existing conversation.** The adapter advertises rc.2's `toolUpdate: 'addition-only'` capability. Both Command Code transports already send the complete active tool declaration list, so the harness can project a tool addition into the current request without a new conversation; tool removals are represented by omission from that active list. This is deliberately not advertised as `in-history`, because the provider wire has no native tool-removal event.

- **The browser follows rc.2 credential updates and safer API-key autofill.** The client now refreshes key state for both `credentials/reference-updated` and rc.2's `credentials/record-updated`, and both Command Code API-key inputs use `autocomplete="new-password"` so browsers are less likely to fill a saved login password into a secret field.

### Fixed

- **CLI-stream cache writes are no longer under-reported.** command-code@1.65.2 added a standalone `cache-write-tokens` JSONL event for requests whose finish usage reports zero or omits `cacheWriteTokens`. The adapter now retains the last finite non-negative reading and uses it under the same zero/missing fallback as the official CLI, including when the finish event carries no `totalUsage`. Positive finish usage still wins, so the event cannot double-count cache writes.

## [0.11.14] - 2026-09-24

### Fixed

- The settings page now delegates remote settings persistence to the DSH Host/gateway instead of silently switching non-loopback pages to a process-local read-only scope. The client always reads `settings.describe` and submits `settings.mutate`; the Host's `writable` fact and the gateway's authenticated write policy remain the authority. This allows an authenticated reverse-proxy deployment to edit settings while keeping unauthorized or Host-locked deployments read-only.

## [0.11.13] - 2026-09-24

### Changed

- **The settings page's save bar only appears when there is something to save.** It used to float over the page at all times with disabled buttons and a separate status bubble. Now a centered capsule rises at the bottom while edits are pending and says what state they are in: unsaved changes, a field that needs fixing, or a failed save. After a save it briefly confirms "Settings saved" with a check mark, then sinks away. The confirmation shows only for a save made while the page is open, and no longer reappears when you switch settings pages or reopen settings. Its Discard and Save are capsule buttons drawn to sit concentrically inside the bar.
- **The settings page is restyled to match the harness's own settings pages.** The card boxes around each section are gone; each group (Accounts, Models, Privacy & security, Integrations & display, Advanced) is now a heading over plain rows separated by hairlines, with the title and description on the left and the control on the right — the layout of the General page. Fonts, sizes and colours use the platform's own scale (14/22 row titles, 12/18 descriptions, 0.5px hairlines, the real error/warning colour aliases), selectors and buttons are the platform's capsules, the auto-approve level is a sliding-pill segmented control like the platform's own, account rows look like the Models page's provider cards (with the same dashed "Add account" button), and the usage figures are filled tiles instead of outlined boxes.
- **An expanded account's usage figures line up in rows.** The four activity tiles sit in one row (two by two in a narrow window) and the three balances in the next, instead of wrapping three to a row with one left over.
- **The AI command guard has one tuning knob: an auto-approve threshold of High, Medium or Low.** The free-form `commandGuardThreshold` probability and the `commandGuardTimeoutMs` budget are gone. `commandGuardLevel` picks High (0.95), Medium (0.9, the default and the old default) or Low (0.8), shown as a three-way switch on the settings page and a select in the dsh-TUI. The decision budget is now fixed at 3 seconds (was 1500 ms by default), since a late verdict only falls back to the normal prompt. A previously stored numeric threshold or timeout is ignored, so a customized threshold returns to Medium.
- **Each account row on the settings page now also shows the monthly credits left**, as a balance after the 5-hour and weekly meters.
- **The Command Code settings page is reorganized around one account list.** Accounts, their usage and their model routing used to live on three separate cards; each account is now one row showing its plan, status and 5-hour/weekly meters, with a **⋯** menu (pin, sign in, replace key, rename, clear key, remove) and an expandable detail with the full usage report and its **dedicated models**. Account operations apply immediately: adding an account no longer needs a page Save before browser sign-in, a failed or cancelled sign-in leaves no empty account behind, and removing an account also drops its key, its dedicated models and a pin naming it. Routing rules are shown as per-account dedicated models (a model belongs to one account at a time; the stored `modelAccountRules` format is unchanged). The remaining settings are grouped into **Models**, **Privacy & security**, **Integrations & display** and a collapsed **Advanced** section, and the working directory is no longer on the page (`workingDir` in config still works).
- **The AI command guard now lets JEV explicitly control narrow sandbox escalations.** Ordinary approvals still use the one-question `safe` protocol. A call carrying `sandboxPermissions` now also asks `escalation_scope` and `escalation_necessity`; all three probabilities must reach the configured threshold before the guard returns `allowed-once`. This lets a conventional build/test command such as `go test ./...` proceed with an outside build cache when JEV agrees that the access is narrow and necessary, while broad, unsupported, malformed, or low-confidence decisions still fall back to the human prompt. The decision memo now retains the two escalation probabilities, and approval logs include them.

## [0.11.12] - 2026-09-24

### Changed

- **This release supports dsh 0.1.7-rc.1 alone, and the two-generation compatibility code is gone.** The plugin has carried a bridge between two Harness generations — the 0.1.2-0.1.6 line (settings.yaml document, `LegacySettingsSchema`, the pre-0.1.7 ApiProxy credential path, `legacy-credentials.ts`) and the 0.1.7 settings world (schema-derived Config forms, volatile fields, the `remote.settings` client scope) — and every release since 0.11.9 has paid for it twice: two schema registrations, two credential paths, a peer range that admitted nine engines and could pair with none of them exactly. This release drops the older line: every `@deepseek-ai/*` peer, `dsh.compatibility.dsh`, `engines.dsh` and the single `dshReleases` record now name `^0.1.7-rc.1` — a range semver resolves to that one release and nothing else — and `@deepseek-ai/schemastery` is tightened from `^3.18.2` to `~3.18.4`, because the unconditional `.volatile()` marking needs schemastery 3.18.3+ and a profile resolving 3.18.2 would mark nothing and render a blank settings form. The bridge itself is deleted, not disabled: `LegacySettingsSchema` and the duplicated config-fields factory (one schema now — every field volatile except the `apiKey` secret), `src/client/legacy-credentials.ts` and its tests, the deprecated `selectCommandCodeSearchProvider()` export (removed in command-code upstream and long superseded by `applyCommandCodeSearchSelection()`), and the legacy branches of the engine-load and install smoke scripts. **If you run dsh 0.1.2–0.1.6, stay on 0.11.11** (the 0.5.0-era Harness line's last release is 0.9.1); both are installed by exact version and unmaintained. On 0.1.7-rc.1 nothing changes behaviorally — same surfaces, same wire — the bundle is simply smaller and the pairing exact. `npm run test:engine` passes against 0.1.7-rc.1 (the published surface links and evaluates there — the durable request-image policy included — and the adapter is driven with the engine's own message constructors), and `npm run test:install` passes the pnpm 10 marketplace-generation smoke against the new peer metadata.

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.65.0 CLI** (2026-09-24 check of the npm `latest`; the official changelog page and RSS still top out at 1.64.0, so the evidence is the bundle diff, the live catalog and the pricing/docs pages). `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` is now `1.65.0`. The release is additive with exactly one change: the CLI registry gains `stealth/space-bunny-alpha` (Space Bunny Alpha) — OpenRouter-served (`chatComplete`), text+image, 1M context, reasoning with the selectable `low`/`medium`/`high` set, available on every plan from Go up, and **free while the stealth preview lasts**. The public catalog serves it too (81 models, 80 + this one), so the picker shows it as `Go · FREE · Image` at $0 rates through the price table's free-model branch — no vendored price row, exactly like the other free models. It is also the **first change to the ZDR exception set since the 1.62.0 check**: the CLI's `modelSupportsZdr`/`knownModelSupportsZdr` negation sets both carry it and the pricing page says it plainly ("Not routed under ZDR"), so `KNOWN_NON_ZDR_MODELS` grows 20 → 21 and `tests/zdr.test.ts` pins the new membership deliberately. Everything else re-verified unchanged: the effort map (one key added, none edited), the Vision set, the plan tiers (no tier moves; the map is 51/58/71/81), the subscription-plan maps, every deal and price, the peak membership and schedule, and the whole wire protocol — every `/alpha/*` and `/provider/v1/*` call site, the `x-command-code-version` / `x-cmd-zdr` header table, the stream vocabulary and the browser-login flow are identical between the 1.64.0 and 1.65.0 bundles apart from minifier renames. The bundle does add a `/alpha/sandbox/*` route family, which belongs to the CLI's own remote-sandbox feature and is never called by this adapter.

## [0.11.11] - 2026-09-23

### Fixed

- **Saving account-rotation edits is visible on a long settings page** ([#60](https://github.com/Mars-Sea/dsh-commandcode-provider/issues/60)). The page's single global Save/Discard bar stays inside the settings panel, attached to the bottom of its scrolling area. A new account can be saved with only a label, then signed in through the same browser authorization flow as the default account; the Host writes the resulting key to that saved account's credential reference. Login attempts for different accounts cannot silently rejoin one another.

- **Command Code responses remain readable when a DSH HTTP dispatcher passes through compressed bytes** ([#59](https://github.com/Mars-Sea/dsh-commandcode-provider/issues/59)). Host-side chat, account usage, catalog, web search, decision, and login requests now ask for `Accept-Encoding: identity`. This also lets a Go account's `403 upgrade_required` body reach the existing CLI-transport fallback. If every usage endpoint answers but its body still cannot be parsed, the account view reports an unreadable response instead of claiming that no request reached the server.

## [0.11.10] - 2026-09-23

### Added

- **Opt-in zero data retention on every chat request.** With `zdr` on (default off), both chat transports send `x-cmd-zdr: 1` on every request. Command Code refuses a model without an available ZDR upstream with `422 cmd_zdr_no_providers`; the plugin explains that error and never retries without the header. `KNOWN_NON_ZDR_MODELS` remains an informational snapshot of the official CLI’s exception list, not a reason to drop protection. The decision endpoint behind the AI command guard has no ZDR upstream and remains a separately documented opt-in. ZDR capacity uses pass-through pricing and can cost more than the ordinary catalog estimate.

- **An opt-in AI command guard: the decision model can approve a safe shell command without interrupting you.** dsh decides *that* a tool call needs approval (permission presets, `PreToolUse` hooks, the sandbox's escalation path) and then asks — there is no second opinion about whether the command is actually safe. With `commandGuard` on, this plugin inserts one answerer in front of the human on the harness's `approval/request` waterfall and asks Command Code's decision model `typesafe/jev` (`POST {apiBase}/provider/v1/systemone`, the same API key and base URL as chat) whether the command is safe to run without asking; only a probability of "safe" at or above `commandGuardThreshold` (default 0.9) becomes a one-shot `allowed-once` grant. **Off by default**, and fail-closed at every step: an unknown call, a non-shell tool, a command longer than 6000 characters (a verdict on a truncated command is a verdict on a different command), a built-in denylist hit (`sudo`, `rm -rf /`, `git push`, publish/upload commands, `curl | sh`, …), a timeout (`commandGuardTimeoutMs`, default 1500 ms), a rate limit, a missing key or an unreadable answer all delegate to the normal prompt. The guard registers only where the approval seam exists (`ctx.inject(['approval'], …)`), reads `tools/pre-execute` purely as a transparent observer (`next()` always — it can never change a decision), and must be prepended on the waterfall because the browser's approval panel otherwise claims the request first. Privacy: the command text, the agent's own description, the working directory and the asker's reason are sent to Command Code, and `typesafe/jev` has **no ZDR upstream** — which is why the feature is opt-in and documented as incompatible with a ZDR-only policy. Billing: free through 2026-09-24, then $0.042/1M input tokens; an identical decision context in the same agent reuses one verdict for 10 minutes, and the timeout also bounds credential lookup. Every decision is logged and lands in the session's own approval audit (`approval/asked`/`approval/decided`).

### New settings

- `commandGuard` (default `false`), `commandGuardThreshold` (0.5–1, default 0.9) and `commandGuardTimeoutMs` (200–10000, default 1500) — editable in the web page's Advanced card, in the dsh-TUI section, or in your profile config.
- `zdr` (default `false`) — zero data retention; editable in the web page's Advanced card, in the dsh-TUI section, or in your profile config.

### Changed

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.64.0 CLI** (2026-09-23 check of the npm `latest`; two releases since the last sync — 1.63.0 and 1.64.0, neither of which has a changelog entry yet, so the evidence is the bundle, the public catalog and the docs). `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` is now `1.64.0`. The train is purely additive: the CLI registry grows 83 → 86 with exactly three additions and no removal or edit, and the public catalog serves 80 models — exactly those three more than the 77 this plugin snapshot covered. `claude-opus-5-5` (Claude Opus 5.5, 1M context, text+image, the five-level `low`/`medium`/`high`/`xhigh`/`max` effort set, `['/messages']` in the catalog so the existing `claude-*` rule keeps it on the CLI transport) is a Provider/Max model — the first since `claude-opus-5` — at $4 / $20 / $0.2 / $5 per 1M, actually cheaper than its predecessor. `gpt-6-sol` (GPT-6 Sol, 1.05M context, same vision and efforts, Pro and above) is $2 / $10 / $0.2 / $2.5 inside a 272K band and $4 / $15 / $0.4 / $5 past it; `gpt-6-luna` (GPT-6 Luna, the opensource-category sibling, on every plan including Go) is $0.1 / $0.5 / $0.01 / $0.125 and $0.2 / $0.75 / $0.02 / $0.25 past the same band. In snapshot terms: the effort map gains three keys (48 → 51), the Vision set three (57 → 60), the plan map one Provider, one Pro and one Go entry without moving a tier (49/57/70/77 → 50/58/71/80), and the vendored price table exactly the three matching rows (76 → 79) with no rate change to any existing row (`node scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs` regenerated the array and cross-checked every rate against the page's rendered table). The same check re-verified unchanged: the subscription plan maps, the ZDR exception set (both CLI anchors extracted from both bundles — the union is 20 members in each, so `meituan/LongCat-2.0` was already excepted at 1.62.0 and the note in `src/capabilities.ts` now says so), the deals, the peak membership and schedule, the automatic-reasoning set, and the whole wire protocol — every `/alpha/*` path, the `x-command-code-version` / `x-cmd-zdr` header table, the stream event vocabulary and the browser-login call site are identical between the two bundles. Two things ARE new upstream and neither changes this adapter's two chat transports. The Provider API documents a fifth endpoint, `POST /provider/v1/systemone` (System One decisions, model `typesafe/jev`, absent from `/provider/v1/models` and never to be listed as a chat model): it is the endpoint the command guard above already speaks, so this sync confirms that contract rather than adding one — its three question types, its 20-question ceiling and its free-through-2026-09-24 window all match the CLI's own `systemOneRequestSchema`. And the docs' deals list carries two temporary "boosted usage" plan-budget promotions (`deepseek-v4.1-flash` and `mimo-v2.6-flash`) that raise a plan's credit allowance rather than any per-token rate — no `deal` object, no price row, nothing in the snapshot.

## [0.11.9] - 2026-09-22

### Changed

- **dsh 0.1.7-alpha.1 is a declared-compatible engine, and the release that rewrote settings needs no user action to keep working.** The engine replaced its settings.yaml document with schema-derived Config forms (`SettingsProvider` → `SettingsForms`, and `installSection` plus the browser's `settingsScope` service are both gone), so this release carries the two-generation adaptation: every Config field except the top-level `apiKey` secret is marked volatile through a feature-detected bridge (`src/config-volatile.ts`) — an unmarked field would be invisible to and unwritable from 0.1.7's settings page — while the ≤0.1.6 registration keeps an unmarked schema and an unwrapped config, exactly as that generation validates and clones it; volatile writes commit in place and re-run the two facts not re-derived per use (the web-search selection and the dsh-TUI section re-registration); and the browser side speaks the `remote.settings` describe/mutate wire directly through the plugin's own scope, so one implementation serves 0.1.2-rc.1 through 0.1.7-alpha.1. The one-time settings.yaml import needs no cooperation from the plugin: the engine looks the section up by profile entry id, which is the same string as the section id (`llm-commandcode`) and must stay that way. `npm run test:engine` passes against 0.1.7-alpha.1 (the bundle links and evaluates there — the request-image policy is the durable one — and the adapter is driven with each engine's own message constructors), so a release installed beside the newest engine is proven to load before it ships.

- **The plugin manager now shows a real identity for this plugin on dsh 0.1.7+.** The package declares `icon` (`icon.svg`) and ships `locale/en.json` + `locale/zh.json` under the `locale/*.json` export, so the plugin-management page renders "Command Code" / "Command Code 提供方" with a description instead of a bare package name and a placeholder icon. Both are pure metadata: older engines ignore them, and every other install path is unaffected.

### Fixed

- **The account card's "无法连接 Command Code 服务 / 请检查网络连接或 API 地址设置" is no longer a guess, and three of its false causes are gone.** `classifyTotalFailure()` answers `network` whenever all four account endpoints throw without a status, so one verdict covered a real outage, a per-request timeout, a key no HTTP header can carry and an unparseable `apiBase` — while the card hid the only evidence, the four per-endpoint messages. Now: (a) the account path validates the key through the harness's own `assertUsableApiKey`, as chat already did, and answers `blocked: 'invalid-key'` with the credential message instead of four phantom transport failures (a key pasted with an embedded newline or a full-width character makes `fetch` throw a `TypeError` **before any I/O**, once per endpoint — the connection was fine); (b) the four endpoints use the connection's own `requestTimeoutMs` (the SAME 60 s budget as a chat call) instead of the catalog's hidden 10 s cap, which on a slow link made every account query time out while chat kept working; (c) both surfaces now render `report.failures`, so the next occurrence names its actual cause. Refresh keeps working in every case — it was the diagnosis, not the fetch lifecycle, that was wrong.

- **Turning the web-search toggle off no longer destroys the user's own `commandcode` pin.** `applyCommandCodeSearchSelection()` remembered "the field already read `commandcode` before we touched it" as `displaced: undefined`, and its disable branch wrote that value back — which is not the no-op its contract promises. The field was cleared, dsh-web reads a cleared `searchProviderId` as auto-select, and with a second usable provider registered (the shipped `deepseek-official` is usable whenever a DeepSeek key resolves) EVERY later search threw `WEB_PROVIDER_AMBIGUOUS`; the same write ran on plugin unload. The tracked selection now carries `preexisting`, so a field we did not take over is left alone, while a field that was genuinely unset at takeover is still cleared (or the toggle would leave Command Code serving). `tests/web-search.test.ts` pins both directions, including an end-to-end boot with `searchProvider: commandcode`.

- **A 5xx that proxies a credits/plan rejection rotates the account instead of being retried as a transient outage.** `classifyAccountRejection()` hoisted only `RATE_LIMITED` above its `status >= 500` guard, so `503 {"code":"INSUFFICIENT_CREDITS"}`, `502 MODEL_NOT_IN_PLAN`, `USAGE_EXCEEDED` and `PREMIUM_CREDITS_EXHAUSTED` were classified as "the provider is unavailable": no rotation, and `SERVER` put the byte-identical request on dsh-llm-retry's 1000-attempt cadence while the configured sibling accounts were never consulted. All four structured codes (`ACCOUNT_UNAVAILABLE_CODES`) are now status-independent, exactly like `RATE_LIMITED`; only the prose scan stays confined to 4xx.

- **A provider-published rate-limit reset of an implausible magnitude no longer breaks the failure it belongs to.** `error.rateLimit.reset` is seconds multiplied by 1000 and was trusted verbatim: `1e300` reached `new Date(1e303).toISOString()` and threw `RangeError: Invalid time value` out of `stream()` in place of the intended retryable `RATE_LIMIT`, and a mark pinned to it became a `cooldown` that no probe ever revisits (the account left rotation for the life of the process). Resets are now accepted only within `MAX_TRUSTED_RESET_MS` (30 days — the longest window this provider meters, so the bound rejects nonsense rather than second-guessing a schedule) at BOTH seams: the rejection body and the `/alpha/billing/credits` probe. A dropped reset degrades the mark to `unknown`, which the probe pass re-checks.

- **An in-band stream error's `statusCode` no longer outvotes a terminal marker or `isRetryable: false`.** `{statusCode: 500, message: 'insufficient credits'}` was reported as retryable `SERVER`, so a permanent quota/plan refusal was replayed on the 1000-attempt cadence. The terminal markers now also match the spaced spelling (the separator is normalized, as the pre-stream classifier already did), and an explicit refusal or a terminal marker outranks a retryable status; an explicit `isRetryable: true` still wins.

- **The first usage report no longer sticks as a false "remote is not mounted" error.** The plugin's `remote.commandcode` namespace lands one wire round-trip after the plugin asks for it, and a surface that asked first (the sidebar quota card does, on every boot) stored the synthetic not-mounted failure as `status: 'error'` — and the card's `shouldRefresh` only fires from `idle`, so nothing retried it until the panel's two-minute tick. The mount callback now re-reads the usage report through a seam declared before the effect (a `const` read in its temporal dead zone would throw). `tests/client-boot.test.ts` boots with the namespace deferred by a macrotask and asserts that re-read.

- **A settings directory whose FIRST read fails retries instead of leaving the page permanently disabled.** With nothing ever held, the mirror keeps its initial `status: 'loading'`/`writable: false` snapshot, which the settings page renders as a fully disabled form behind a "read-only" banner — and the forwarded invalidations need a live Host to fire, so a Host that was still starting up left the page dead until a reload. `SettingsDescribeMirror` now retries a first-read failure on a bounded 1/2/4 s ladder (`SETTINGS_DESCRIBE_RETRY_MS`, injectable timer, cancelled on disposal); a failure with a HELD view schedules nothing, because the page is already showing the last good document.

- **A concurrent `loginBegin` no longer leaks a loopback login server.** `begin()` rejoin read a status that only becomes `waiting` after two awaits, so two GUI tabs (or a Sign-in following a cancel that arrived too early to retire anything) each bound their own server — and only the last one was reachable by `teardown()`, so the orphan kept listening and answering `/callback` for the process's lifetime; ten of those exhausted the login port window. `begin()` is now single-flight across the bind, a `cancel()` in that window sets `cancelPending` (observed after the bind, which closes the fresh server and publishes `failed/cancelled`), and `bindServer` closes a server it does not track.

- **`DSH_COMMANDCODE_TRACE=false` (or `0`/`no`/`off`) now disables the trace instead of enabling it.** Only `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` were recognized as flags and everything else was taken for a path, so an explicit disable switched the trace ON and wrote the conversation to a file named `false` in the process's working directory.

- **DSH 0.1.7 tool results survive into the next model request.** That engine uses `role: 'tool'` with message-level `toolCallId` and raw content blocks; the adapter only recognized the older user-role `tool-result` wrapper and silently discarded both results and their paired calls on both transports. The model consequently lost completed work, could repeat tools, and could end with only a statement of intent. Both envelopes now share pairing and serialization, including errors, images and long call ids. Engine smoke tests capture both request bodies using each engine's actual message constructors.

- **Reasoning-only replies no longer silently complete a turn.** Both transports now validate that a terminal response contains answer text or a tool call before emitting a success finish. Empty completions use the existing `EMPTY_RESPONSE` retry policy; output-limit completions without an answer report non-retryable `OUTPUT_TOKEN_LIMIT` with the actual budget and available usage, without assuming all tokens went to reasoning. Received usage is retained on failure, and trace terminal diagnostics survive the payload cap.

- **A reply cut off mid-generation is a reported failure again, not a turn that quietly finished** (report: 《调用会莫名其妙的自动停止，并没有任何报错信息》). The mechanism was in the stream tail: when the response body ended without its transport's terminal event — the gateway or an intermediary closing the socket mid-generation — the adapter closed the open blocks and yielded a clean `finish: { kind: 'stop' }`. Both transports DO terminate explicitly (the CLI transport with a `finish` event, the Provider API with a final `finish_reason` chunk), so an EOF there is not a normal ending, and reporting it as one is exactly why a truncated answer arrived with no error in the UI and nothing in the session log: the harness saw a completed turn. The tail now classifies. `!sawContent` (no visible text AND no tool call) keeps the pre-existing retryable `EMPTY_RESPONSE`, because such a request is free to repeat and the retry policy's whitelist absorbs it — a cut during a long thinking phase lands there, unchanged. A cut that HAD produced content throws `STREAM_CLOSED`, the code `dsh-llm-deepseek`'s own `translate()` raises for the same condition ("… stream ended before message_stop"); a tool call still in flight when the socket closed is dropped rather than executed with truncated JSON arguments. The code is deliberately OUTSIDE `providerRetryPolicy()`'s whitelist for now: every retry of a content-bearing cut re-generates the whole answer, so auto-retrying is a cost decision rather than an obvious win (adding it to `retryableCodes`, ideally bounded by `src/transport-retry.ts`'s per-agent budget, is the one-line opt-in). `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins both endings on both transports, the in-flight-tool-call refusal, and the untouched `EMPTY_RESPONSE` path.
- **The Command Code settings page is interactive again on dsh 0.1.7** (report: 《Command Code 设置页面所有的按钮和开关都无法点击，下拉选择模型也无法使用》). The page rendered — with its read-only banner, empty text fields and every switch, button and select disabled — because the plugin's own settings scope read `ctx.remote.settings` directly, and `remote.settings` is a Cordis service NESTED under the `remote` service (api-gateway registers one `Service` per contribution namespace as `remote.<ns>`): a read from a fiber that does not declare that name in its inject list is answered with `Error: cannot get property "remote.settings" without inject`. The throw landed inside the scope's own `try`/`catch`, so nothing was logged and no `settings/describe` ever left the browser; the mirror stayed `idle`, the scope kept its INITIAL snapshot (`status: 'loading'`, `writable: false`, `value: undefined`), and since the page disables its controls on `!state.writable`, everything was inert. The boolean toggles were the confusing part of the report: they fall back to their hardcoded `defaultChecked` while the scope has no value, so they looked "set" over empty text inputs. `createSettingsScope()` now takes the namespace as a resolver, and the client entry fills it from the object `ctx.inject(['remote.settings'], …)` resolves — the same shape the `remote.credentials` and `remote.commandcode` reads have always used. `$host` / `$on` / `$mount` are plain members of the declared `remote` service, so those stay direct reads, and no namespace was added to the exported client `inject` list: gating the whole client half on a service a profile may not serve would silence every surface, which is the failure this scope is built to avoid. Verified end to end against a real dsh 0.1.7-alpha.1 web app (the page converges on the stored row, a toggle marks it dirty, the model multi-select opens its 76-model list, and a save lands in the profile document and survives a reload); `tests/settings-scope.test.ts` now builds its fake context with a `settings` getter that throws the engine's exact error and covers the unmounted → mount + `refresh()` handshake, and `tests/client-boot.test.ts` mounts its namespaces as real `remote.<ns>` Cordis services — with the old direct read restored, the boot suite fails.

### Added

- **`DSH_COMMANDCODE_TRACE=<path>`: an opt-in raw-stream trace, so the next "it stopped in the middle" report arrives with evidence** (`src/stream-trace.ts`). A cut stream is diagnosed from the wire or not at all — which events actually arrived, how long the socket then sat silent, and how it ended are the facts that separate "the provider closed it" from "our parser missed the terminal event", and until now the adapter kept none of them. With the variable set (a bare `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` writes to `$TMPDIR/dsh-commandcode-stream.jsonl`), every request appends JSONL: one record per raw chunk carrying the provider's own payload verbatim, plus `response` / `eof` / `idle-timeout` / `read-error` / `end` / `stream-close`, each stamped with milliseconds since that request's first byte. Records never include the request body, an API key, or a request header; one request is capped at 4 MiB (`trace-capped` names the cap); a path that cannot be written turns the trace off rather than failing the request it is observing; and unset, the whole facility is one environment read and some inert calls. `scripts/probe-stream.mjs` is the companion isolation test — it drives the adapter alone, with no harness and no session, so a cut seen there is the provider's while a clean `finished` puts the cut above the adapter (`PROBE_MODEL` / `PROBE_MAX_TOKENS` / `PROBE_PROMPT` / `PROBE_TRACE` override its defaults). `tests/stream-trace.test.ts` pins switch resolution, the JSONL shape, the cap, the degradation paths, and the adapter wiring on both a finished and a cut stream. Both live probes so far (`deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, 13 and 244 chunks) reached `finished` on `/provider/v1/chat/completions`, so the gateway does not cut unconditionally.

## [0.11.8] - 2026-09-22

This train is purely additive: the CLI registry grows 78 → 83 with exactly five additions and no removal or edit, so five models gain their picker annotations and prices. `xai/grok-4.7` (500K context, text+image, `low`/`medium`/`high`/`xhigh` efforts, GOAT and above) launches on a 40% off deal running 2026-09-21 → 2026-09-27 that reverts to $2.00 in / $6.00 out when it lapses; its snapshotted rates are the discounted $1.20 in / $3.60 out / $0.30 cache read, doubling to $2.40 / $7.20 / $0.60 past 200K, and the deal carries the page's own expiry stamp so the badge hides itself after that instant. `stepfun/Step-5-Preview` is StepFun's 600B sparse-MoE agentic coding model (1M context, text+image, `low`/`medium`/`high` efforts, Go, $1.00 / $2.70 / $0.05). In snapshot terms: the effort map gains exactly two keys (46 → 48), the Vision set the five (52 → 57), the plan map three Go and two GOAT entries without moving a tier (46/52/65/72 → 49/57/70/77), the deals five → six, and the vendored price table exactly the five matching rows (71 → 76) with no rate change to any existing row (`node scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs --check` now reports all 76 rows up to date). The two upstream leftovers stay deliberately un-snapshotted, exactly as the previous check recorded them: `gpt-6-astra` is still a CLI/pricing/docs-only model the public catalog does not serve, and the pricing page still embeds an already-expired (`2026-06-22`) `qwen-3.7-max-2x-usage` deal record its own Deals section does not list.

## [0.11.7] - 2026-09-20

### Fixed
- **A plain 429 is no longer reported as an exhausted usage window** ([#54](https://github.com/Mars-Sea/dsh-commandcode-provider/issues/54)). The report was a contradiction: the turn failed with `llm-commandcode: all 1 Command Code account(s) have exhausted their usage window` while the account card at that moment showed the five-hour window at 2% ($0.22 / $14.00) and the weekly one at 10% ($3.43 / $35.00), and the reporter noted it only reproduced at the default reasoning effort and always recovered by itself a little later. The message was the tell: it carried no "the earliest window resets at …" clause, and that clause appears only when a mark is a `cooldown` with a known reset — so the pool was reporting an exhausted window from a mark that had no reset at all, i.e. from nothing but a 429. The chain: `classifyAccountRejection()` treated ANY 429 (or a bare `RATE_LIMITED` code) as a usage-window rejection, `markRejected()` filed it as `unknown`, the follow-up `/alpha/billing/credits` probe either failed (the same account was still being refused) or read no reset, and `allAccountsUnusable()` then asserted "exhausted their usage window" regardless of the evidence — exactly the sentence the reporter was shown. Classification now follows the official CLI's own bar (command-code@1.56.0 `parseWindowLimitError` → `resolveWindowLabel`): a rejection is a WINDOW limit only when the body names one — `error.rateLimit.window` of `fiveHour`/`weekly`/`daily`, or the "usage limit for your plan" wording — and `readWindowLimitEvidence()` reads that window and its `reset` (seconds, with the `resets at <ISO>` wording as the fallback) in one place. Everything else stays a new `throttled` reason: it rotates and marks the key exactly like a window limit does (so rotation across a multi-account pool is unchanged, and a burst or model-level limiter still moves the request off that key), but the mark carries a `cause` — `window`, `throttle` or `auth` — and only a `window` cause may be described as an exhausted window. A throttle mark is deliberately left `unknown` with no reset, so the next all-marked pass still probes the real windows and upgrades the cause to `window` when a probe reads one as exceeded; a throttle mark that a probe cannot confirm now answers `all N Command Code account(s) are rate limited (429) — the provider did not report an exhausted usage window; retrying` instead, with no invented reset and no invented wait. The same honesty was applied to a window mark whose provider published no reset (`— the provider published no reset time for it` / `，服务商未公布重置时间`), where the old message silently said nothing, and the adapter's end-of-turn `RATE_LIMIT` for a non-429 status carrying a bare `RATE_LIMITED` now says the same thing. `tests/accounts.test.ts` pins the throttle diagnosis (and that it never claims a window), the probe upgrade from throttle to window, the reset-stamped window cooldown, and the no-reset wording; `tests/adapter.test.ts` pins the classification of a bare 429 as `throttled`, the three window-carrying shapes as `rate-limit` (including the plan-usage-limit wording and a window without a reset), and the end-of-turn message.

### Changed

- **Synced with the official command-code@1.58.0 CLI** (2026-09-20 check of the 2026-09-19 npm `latest`, whose changelog entry is "Retire the free LongCat 2.0 tier and sell LongCat 2.0 as a paid model"). `COMMAND_CODE_CLI_VERSION` is now `1.58.0`. The CLI registry grows 77 → 78 with exactly one addition — the paid `meituan/LongCat-2.0` — while its retired `meituan/LongCat-2.0:free` sibling gains `hidden` and the display name "LongCat 2.0 (Free)": that is the CLI catching up with the backend rename the 1.57.0 sync had already snapshotted, so no capability-snapshot entry moves, the model was already served under the paid id at $0.30 in / $1.20 out / $0.006 cache read on Go, and no runtime behavior changes. Everything else is re-verified unchanged against both the bundle and the public sources: the per-model effort map, the subscription plan maps (8 plans including their monthly credits), every plan tier (map still 46/52/65/72), the five model deals, the peak/off-peak membership and schedule (01:00–04:00 and 06:00–10:00 UTC, Monday–Friday only), the vendored price table (`node scripts/sync-model-prices.mjs --check` reports all 71 rows up to date), and the public catalog (71 models, its `supported_endpoints` mix still 55 × chat/completions + responses, the same 8 Claude ids × messages-only, 8 × chat/completions, which agrees with the adapter's `claude-*` routing rule). Static inspection of both bundles finds no transport drift: the `/alpha/*` endpoint set, the `/alpha/generate` converters (`toWireMessages`/`toWireTools`/`toWireToolOutput`), the stream event vocabulary, the `x-command-code-version` header, the browser-login call site and the web-search field names are byte-identical to 1.57.0, and the documented Provider API endpoint set is unchanged. Two upstream leftovers stay deliberately un-snapshotted, exactly as the previous check recorded them: `gpt-6-astra` is still a CLI/pricing/docs-only model the public catalog does not serve, and the pricing page still embeds an already-expired (`2026-06-22`) `qwen-3.7-max-2x-usage` deal record that its own Deals section does not list. The Provider API docs now also document the optional `x-cmd-zdr: 1` zero-data-retention header (the CLI parity of `CMD_ZDR=1`); this plugin does not send it, because ZDR opt-in would be a new feature rather than a data sync.

## [0.11.6] - 2026-09-19

### Fixed
- **An account the user explicitly selected comes back on its own** ([#51](https://github.com/Mars-Sea/dsh-commandcode-provider/issues/51)). With several accounts, pinning one in the **Active account** dropdown and then hitting a 429 on it moved every later request to `default` for the rest of the process, and re-selecting the account in the settings page could not bring it back — only restarting dsh did (the report's 被切换后需要重启 dsh 才能换回来). The mechanism: a 429 marks the key `unknown`, which only a `/alpha/billing/credits` window probe can clear, and the pool's only probe pass runs once EVERY account is marked — a state that never arrives while another account can still serve.
